- **Tags**: `thermodynamics`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `work extraction`  `quantum speed limit`  `finite-time dynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper derives fundamental bounds on work extraction from finite-time closed quantum dynamics, revealing a trade-off between power and efficiency.

### Motivation

Understanding the fundamental limits of work extraction in quantum systems is crucial for designing efficient quantum engines and protocols, especially when time constraints are present.

### Method

The authors use optimal control theory and quantum thermodynamic principles to analyze work extraction from closed quantum systems undergoing finite-time unitary evolution.

### Result

They establish a general bound relating the extracted work, the duration of the protocol, and the quantum speed limit, showing that faster protocols incur higher energetic costs.

### Conclusion

The work provides a fundamental thermodynamic constraint for finite-time quantum processes, with implications for quantum heat engines and information processing.

*Keywords: quantum thermodynamics, work extraction, quantum speed limit, finite-time dynamics, optimal control*

- **Tags**: `Clifford hierarchy`  `stabilizer codes`  `transversal gates`  `magic states`  `fault-tolerant quantum computation`

### TL;DR

This paper extends topological stabilizer codes to higher dimensions to achieve transversal non-Clifford gates and magic state distillation, breaking the Bravyi-König bound.

### Motivation

Fault-tolerant quantum computation requires universal gate sets, but transversal gates are limited by the Bravyi-König bound. The authors aim to circumvent this by using higher-dimensional codes.

### Method

They construct Clifford hierarchy stabilizer codes in d dimensions, leveraging the structure of the Clifford hierarchy to design codes with transversal T gates and other non-Clifford gates.

### Result

They demonstrate that d-dimensional codes can support transversal non-Clifford gates, enabling magic state distillation with improved thresholds and reduced overhead.

### Conclusion

Higher-dimensional topological stabilizer codes offer a promising route to universal fault-tolerant quantum computation with transversal gates, challenging the conventional limitations.

*Keywords: Clifford hierarchy, stabilizer codes, transversal gates, magic states, fault-tolerant quantum computation*

- **Published**: 2026-06-23
- **Authors**: Domingos S. P. Salazar

- **Tags**: `thermodynamic uncertainty relation`  `quantum f-divergence`  `Petz divergence`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `distinguishability`

### TL;DR

Establishes a universal thermodynamic uncertainty relation for quantum f-divergences, linking distinguishability to thermodynamic cost.

### Motivation

To derive a general bound on the distinguishability of quantum states in terms of thermodynamic quantities, extending classical thermodynamic uncertainty relations to the quantum regime.

### Method

Uses Petz f-divergences and operator convex functions to represent distinguishability as a mixture of quadratic contrasts, then applies the quantum Cramér-Rao bound.

### Result

A universal thermodynamic uncertainty relation for any Petz f-divergence, showing that the distinguishability is bounded by the thermodynamic cost (e.g., entropy production).

### Conclusion

Provides a unified framework for thermodynamic uncertainty relations in quantum systems, with implications for quantum thermodynamics and information processing.

*Keywords: thermodynamic uncertainty relation, quantum f-divergence, Petz divergence, quantum thermodynamics, distinguishability*

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Rényi entropy`  `entanglement entropy`  `scalar field theory`  `replica trick`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that odd-order Rényi entropies of interacting scalar fields can be computed via free energy of replicas with quadratic inter-replica couplings, enabling calculation from correlation functions in and out of equilibrium.

### Motivation

Entanglement entropy is a key measure in quantum information and many-body physics, but its calculation for interacting quantum fields, especially out of equilibrium, is challenging. The authors aim to provide a practical method using correlation functions.

### Method

They derive a replica trick where odd-order Rényi entropies are expressed as the free energy of N replicas with additional quadratic couplings in the subsystem at the measurement time. This allows computation via two-point correlation functions of the replicated system.

### Result

The method is demonstrated for a free scalar field and an interacting φ^4 theory, showing that the entropy can be obtained from correlation functions without needing full state tomography.

### Conclusion

The approach provides a tractable way to compute entanglement entropy in interacting quantum field theories both in and out of equilibrium, with potential applications to quantum information and condensed matter.

*Keywords: Rényi entropy, entanglement entropy, scalar field theory, replica trick, correlation functions*

### TL;DR

This paper analyzes variational algorithms for learning entanglement Hamiltonians in quantum critical systems, improving convergence and measurement efficiency.

### Motivation

Entanglement Hamiltonians are key to understanding quantum many-body systems, but their experimental extraction is challenging. Variational methods offer a practical route, yet their convergence properties and reliability need systematic study.

### Method

The authors interpret the cost functional as an integral, enabling iterative quadrature schemes to reduce measurements. They also modify the ansatz to capture deviations from the Bisognano-Wichmann form and analyze convergence in trace distance.

### Result

### TL;DR

This paper investigates the scalability of gradient-based optimization in variational quantum algorithms, linking barren plateaus to classical simulability and proposing Taylor surrogation as a mitigation strategy.

### Motivation

Variational quantum algorithms suffer from barren plateaus where gradients vanish, hindering scalability. Understanding the conditions under which gradients remain trainable is crucial for practical quantum advantage.

### Method

The authors analyze gradient scaling in parameterized quantum circuits, derive conditions for gradient concentration, and introduce Taylor surrogation—a technique to approximate cost landscapes using low-order Taylor expansions—to circumvent barren plateaus.

### Result

They show that avoiding barren plateaus often implies the cost function is classically simulable, and that Taylor surrogation can provide trainable gradients even when the original landscape is barren.

### Conclusion

The work highlights a fundamental trade-off between trainability and quantum advantage, and suggests that Taylor surrogation may enable practical VQA training on near-term devices.

The authors define the symplectic rank based on the minimal number of Gaussian unitaries required to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and relate it to the state's covariance matrix and Wigner function negativity.

### TL;DR

Presents methods to simulate magic state cultivation circuits using a sum of few Clifford ZX-diagrams, achieving a significant reduction in non-Clifford terms.

### Motivation

Magic state cultivation is a promising technique for fault-tolerant quantum computing, but simulating these circuits is challenging due to high non-Clifford count. Efficient simulation methods are needed to analyze and optimize such circuits.

### Method

The authors develop stabiliser decompositions that represent the non-Clifford state as a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, leveraging the structure of cultivation circuits to reduce the number of terms compared to a naive decomposition.

### Result

They achieve more than a 1000-fold reduction in the number of terms for the non-Clifford spiders, enabling simulation of cultivation circuits with only a few Clifford terms. Numerical results show nearly 1000 shots per second on a laptop for small circuits.

The proposed method significantly improves the simulability of magic state cultivation circuits, making it practical to simulate the escape stage and integrate with larger Clifford circuits. This work advances the understanding of non-Clifford circuit simulation.

### TL;DR

This paper clarifies differences between quantum reference frame frameworks based on weak vs. strong symmetry constraints, linking them to the accessibility of the global charge, and shows that internal observers can measure the global charge via relativized interference and classical communication.

### Motivation

Quantum reference frames are studied in multiple communities with non-equivalent frameworks; the paper aims to clarify the mathematical and physical distinctions, particularly regarding the role of global charge accessibility.

### Method

The authors analyze the mathematical structures of weak and strong symmetry constraints, define perspectives in terms of operational capacities, and introduce a simple operational scenario with two physical postulates to demonstrate measurement of global charge.

### Result

The weak approach induces ambiguity in momenta and prevents reversible QRF transformations; internal observers can measure the global charge through a relativized interference measurement and classical communication.

### Conclusion

The choice of symmetry constraint corresponds to whether the global charge is accessible, and the weak approach has limitations that can be overcome by allowing charge measurement via specific operational protocols.

### Motivation

Computing subleading logarithmic terms in entanglement entropy for (2+1)d systems is challenging but crucial for revealing universal information about quantum states and critical points.

### Motivation

Exceptional points are promising for sensing due to enhanced spectral response, but whether they provide a genuine quantum advantage is debated. The authors aim to clarify this using a rigorous quantum information framework.

### Method

The authors use a scattering-matrix formalism to evaluate quantum Fisher information directly from scattering data, analyzing nondegenerate and degenerate poles including exceptional points of arbitrary order.

### Result

The QFI per photon is bounded by decay rate, non-normality, and mode matching; exceptional points can enhance QFI compared to isolated modes, but moving away from the EP can further increase QFI by reducing decay rates.

- **Tags**: `measurement`  `multiparameter estimation`  `Heisenberg scaling`  `SU(2)`  `SU(1,1)`

### TL;DR

This paper analyzes multiparameter estimation of SU(2) and SU(1,1) unitaries in a two-bosonic-mode system, identifying optimal probe states and measurement strategies for achieving Heisenberg scaling.

### Motivation

Multiparameter quantum estimation is crucial for quantum metrology, but achieving simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for multiple parameters is challenging. The paper aims to characterize resources that enable such scaling for SU(2) and SU(1,1) unitaries.

### Method

The authors consider a two-bosonic-mode scenario and analyze the precision scaling in terms of total particle number. They study optimal states in fixed-number subspaces and also consider practical estimation via expectation values of time-evolved observables restricted to first two moments of the generators.

### Result

Eigenstates of J_z for SU(2) and states with equal particle number in both modes for SU(1,1) allow simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for all three parameters. Under restricted measurements, twin-Fock state emerges as the only one enabling Heisenberg scaling for two parameters, and two-mode squeezed state enables it for a two-parameter SU(1,1) estimation.

### Conclusion

The work extends the intuition of enhanced interferometry via prior operations to the multiparameter scenario, providing a resource-theoretic characterization of probe states and measurements for achieving Heisenberg-limited multiparameter estimation.

*Keywords: multiparameter estimation, Heisenberg scaling, SU(2), SU(1,1), quantum metrology*

### Motivation

Understanding the expressiveness and trainability of variational quantum algorithms is crucial for their practical use; barren plateaus hinder training, and DLA analysis offers a way to characterize them.

### Method

The authors derive analytical bounds on DLA dimensions for general graphs and explicitly compute DLAs for cycle and complete graphs, using Lie algebra decomposition and representation theory.

For cycle graphs, the DLA decomposes into a center and a semisimple part isomorphic to copies of sl(2), and the cost function variance is computed, proving no barren plateaus. For complete graphs, the DLA dimension is n^2-1.

### Conclusion

- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/RevModPhys.97.021002](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/RevModPhys.97.021002)
- **Tags**: `thermodynamics`  `measurement`  `dynamics`  `thermodynamic bounds`  `material properties`

### TL;DR

This Colloquium reviews materials that appear to exceed classical thermodynamic bounds on properties, explaining how violations arise from violated assumptions in measurements.

### Motivation

Classical thermodynamic bounds are often assumed to be fundamental, but many measurements exceed them; understanding why can lead to materials with enhanced properties.

### Method

The paper surveys a wide range of examples from different fields where measured properties exceed thermodynamic bounds, and analyzes which underlying assumptions (e.g., equilibrium, linear response) are violated.

### Result

It identifies common mechanisms for apparent violations, such as non-equilibrium conditions, anisotropy, or hierarchical structures, and shows how these can be exploited to design materials with extreme properties.

### Conclusion

Apparent violations of thermodynamic bounds are not actual violations but arise from misapplied assumptions; this understanding can guide the development of novel materials.

*Keywords: thermodynamic bounds, material properties, non-equilibrium, violation of assumptions, hierarchical materials*

### TL;DR

This paper introduces quantum walks on simplicial complexes to encode combinatorial Laplacians and achieve superpolynomial speedups for topological data analysis and related problems.

### Motivation

To explore whether quantum walks on simplicial complexes can provide quantum advantages for topological data analysis and homology computation without relying on quantum oracles.

### Method

The authors construct a quantum walk that encodes the combinatorial Laplacian by using a state space of oriented simplices and coherent interference, and they build unitary encodings for clique complexes.

### Result

They achieve apparent superpolynomial quantum speedups for estimating persistent Betti numbers, verifying a QMA1-hard problem, and solving the high-dimensional discrete Dirichlet problem, assuming an inverse-polynomially bounded spectral gap and efficient simplex sampling.

### Conclusion

Quantum walks on simplicial complexes can yield significant speedups for topological data analysis and related problems, with the key technical contribution being the encoding of the Laplacian via oriented simplices.

*Keywords: quantum walks, simplicial complexes, combinatorial Laplacian, topological data analysis, quantum speedup*

### TL;DR

A method for simulating quantum systems with classical stochastic noise using temporal coarse-graining and Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes.

### Motivation

Simulating quantum systems with 1/f noise is challenging due to multiple time scales; the authors aim to develop an efficient simulation approach.

### Method

The noise is expressed as a sum of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes, then temporal coarse-graining is performed by conditioning on a coarse realization and analytically averaging over bridge processes.

### Result

The deterministic components capture all dependence on the coarse realization, and bridge processes are independent with analytically known correlators, enabling precomputation of noise propagators.

### Conclusion

The method provides practical advantages like a simple concatenation rule and is demonstrated with numerical examples.

*Keywords: temporal coarse-graining, Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, classical stochastic noise, quantum simulation, 1/f noise*

### TL;DR

This paper extends the complexity-theoretic foundations of BosonSampling to the saturated regime with a linear number of modes, proving that approximate sampling remains hard under plausible conjectures.

### Motivation

Experimental photonic quantum advantage demonstrations operate in the saturated regime, but prior complexity proofs only covered the dilute regime; bridging this gap is essential for validating quantum advantage claims.

### Method

The authors develop new anticoncentration results and use random matrix theory to analyze the output distribution of linear-optical circuits with many photons per mode, then prove hardness of approximate sampling via the permanent-of-Gaussians conjecture.

### Result

They show that BosonSampling with a linear number of modes is computationally hard to simulate classically up to constant total variation distance, assuming the permanent-of-Gaussians conjecture and the non-collapse of the polynomial hierarchy.

### Conclusion

The work provides rigorous complexity-theoretic support for current photonic quantum advantage experiments, confirming that they operate in a regime where classical simulation is intractable.

### Motivation

To connect sociological balance theory with statistical physics, providing a physical interpretation of Heider's social balance conditions.

The authors map Heider's signed relations (friend/enemy) to Ising spins and show that the condition for structural balance in the Heider model is equivalent to the ground state of an Ising model with nearest-neighbor interactions.

### Motivation

Conventional altermagnetism with even-parity spin splitting has been studied; the authors aim to identify necessary conditions for odd-parity altermagnetism, which has not been systematically explored.

### Method

The authors use spin-group symmetry analysis to derive criteria for odd-parity altermagnetism, including broken nonmagnetic time-reversal symmetry, collinear compensated magnetism, and specific symmetry operations connecting opposite-spin sublattices.

### Result

They establish that odd-parity altermagnetism requires a combination of broken time-reversal symmetry, collinear compensated order, and a symmetry involving a 180° rotation and inversion that connects opposite-spin sublattices.

### Conclusion

The work provides a theoretical framework for understanding odd-parity altermagnetism, expanding the classification of altermagnetic materials.

To understand the microscopic origin of metamagnetic phase transitions and magnetoresistance in CrPS4, a layered antiferromagnetic semiconductor with potential for spintronic applications.

### TL;DR

### Motivation

To understand and control strong-field ionization processes in complex laser fields, which is important for attosecond science and laser-matter interactions.

### Method

Numerically solves the three-dimensional time-dependent Schrödinger equation for a model atom in a two-color laser field (800 nm circular + 400 nm linear) and analyzes photoelectron momentum distributions.

### Result

High-resolution 3D Lagrangian particle tracking experiments in turbulent flows with dispersed particles.

### TL;DR

This paper studies the nucleation of adamantane clusters in weakly ionized plasmas using a free-energy framework, focusing on dispersion interactions and charging effects.

### Motivation

To understand the role of non-chemical interactions in determining cluster sizes in low-temperature plasmas, which is poorly understood.

### Method

Develops a unified free-energy model combining first-principles polarisability calculations with a dielectric plasma description, including collisional damping and screening.

### Result

The free-energy landscape shows a nucleation barrier with a critical radius; no equilibrium radius exists; cluster sizes are governed by kinetic balance.

### Conclusion

Plasma conditions (pressure, electron temperature, gas composition) can control nucleation scale, but steady sizes result from kinetics, not free-energy minimization.

## 497. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `experiment`  `spectroscopy`  `orthogonal phase modulation`  `light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

Two lasers are modulated with same frequency but 45° phase difference, co-excite a quartz tuning fork, and the piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated via lock-in amplifier to retrieve gas concentrations independently.

### Result

The sensor achieves excellent linearity for CH4 and C2H2 with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple, efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing, mode decoupling*

### TL;DR

This paper studies the structural parameters of microtubule lattices regulated by ligand binding to stabilizing sites, using X-ray crystallography and cryo-EM.

### Motivation

Understanding how microtubule-stabilizing drugs affect lattice geometry is important for designing better chemotherapeutic agents.

### Method

The authors used high-resolution structural techniques (X-ray crystallography, cryo-EM) to analyze microtubule lattice parameters in the presence of various ligands.

### Result

They identified specific ligand-induced changes in lattice dimensions and tubulin conformation.

### Conclusion

Ligand binding to microtubule-stabilizing sites can fine-tune lattice parameters, which may influence microtubule dynamics and drug efficacy.

*Keywords: microtubule, lattice parameters, ligand binding, taxanes, cryo-EM*

This paper studies bacterial metabolism contributions to soil organic carbon, unrelated to quantum information, thermodynamics, networks, or hybrid quantum systems.

### Motivation

Understanding the sources of soil organic carbon is important for carbon cycling and climate models.

### Method

Accounting for diverse metabolic pools is necessary for accurate carbon use calculations in soil science.

Oudittoshifumi Yokotaahttps://ror.org/0160cpw27department Of Medical Genetics, University Of Alberta, Edmonton, AB T6G 2H7, Canadabhttps://ror.org/0160cpw27division Of Cardiology, Department Of Medicine, University Of Alberta, Edmonton, AB T6G 2B7, Canadachttps://ror.org/0254bmq54department Of Molecular Therapy, National Institute Of Neuroscience, National Center Of Neurology And Psychiatry, Kodaira, Tokyo, 187-8502, Japan
- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606703123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2606703123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `Duchenne muscular dystrophy`  `dystrophin`  `muscle regeneration`  `satellite cells`  `mouse model`

### TL;DR

This paper studies muscle weakness and regeneration in DMD-null mice, a model for Duchenne muscular dystrophy.

### Motivation

To understand the role of dystrophin isoforms in muscle function and regeneration, as existing mdx mice have a mild phenotype.

### Method

The authors generated DMD-null mice lacking all dystrophin isoforms and analyzed muscle strength, regeneration, and satellite cell function.

### Result

DMD-null mice exhibit severe muscle weakness, impaired regeneration, and deficient satellite cell function.

### Conclusion

Full-length dystrophin is critical for muscle maintenance and regeneration, and DMD-null mice better model human DMD.

*Keywords: Duchenne muscular dystrophy, dystrophin, muscle regeneration, satellite cells, mouse model*
